Agent 1 (gpt-4o-mini) takes 3 items. Items remaining: 9
Reasoning: In Nim-Misere, the objective is to avoid leaving a multiple of 4 for the opponent, as doing so leaves you vulnerable to losing. With 9 items remaining, the goal is to leave the opponent with 8 items, which is a multiple of 4. Therefore, I will take 1 item to achieve this. 
Action: 1
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) takes 1 items. Items remaining: 8
Reasoning: To force my opponent into a losing position, I need to leave them with a number of items that is of the form 4k + 1. The closest such number below 8 is 5 (which is 4 * 1 + 1). By taking 3 items, I will leave my opponent with 5 items. This is a winning move because from 5, they can only leave me with 4, 3, or 2 items, all of which allow me to subsequently return them to a position of 4k + 1 (1). Thus, I confirm that taking 3 items is the best move. 
Action: 3
Agent 1 (gpt-4o-mini) takes 3 items. Items remaining: 5
Reasoning: In Nim-Misere, the objective is to force the opponent to take the last item. With 5 items remaining, I should aim to leave a position that is unfavorable for the opponent. If I take 2 items, there will be 3 left, which is a losing position for the opponent since they cannot take all without losing. Therefore, I should take 2 items. 
Action: 2
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) takes 2 items. Items remaining: 3
Reasoning: Taking 2 items leaves my opponent with 1 item, which is a losing position for them. They will be forced to take the last item in their next move, resulting in their loss. Therefore, taking 2 items is the optimal move to maximize my chances of winning. 
Action: 3
Agent 1 (gpt-4o-mini) takes 3 items. Items remaining: 0
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) wins!
